                                                    FLYING_DISCS

             The "Tacona News Tribune" and through them the United Press put out
 a story that an army plane which was allegedly carrying parts     of a disc which
 had  struck  a boat owed  by Harold Dahl and  Fred Chrisman had  crashed  on August

 1, 1947, killing two Air Force Intelligence     officers who                          were     interviewing
 persons who were   alleged to have seen flying discs.
                     Herold 4=
                      Dahl and Fred    Chrisman, when interviewed by Bureau Agents,
 advised       signed statement
               in     a            on August 7, 1947 ,
                                                     that   in the early part of June,
 1947 , they picked up sone strange rock formations    from a gravel   pit on Mauri
 Island, Washington .  They sent a cigar box of these formations      to one Ray PaIner ,
 editor of                     gazine in Evanston, Illinois
                     the Venture         ma                             and also    editor    of the
 Fantasy magazinein Chicago           Illinois.       According     to  them   they  requested     Palmer
 to make only       chemical   ana lysis      of the rock    formations        Falmer then wrote
 asking for additional samples stating he had been unable to analyze the material.
 Dahl and    Chrisman renarked     that 2 few days after the flying disc                 stories appeared
 during   the  latter part of June, Falmer          contacted    then by telephone saying he would
 pej for an exclusive    story if the materials they had sent him were fragnents of
 a flying disc.     Dahl said he   wrote  Palmer   letter in which    he represented the
 material   as being 4 part    of 2 flying disc , and both Dahl    and Chrisman  aditted
 that this           statement                entirely false .
            Dahl and Chrisman then received                  call fron one Kenneth Arnold of
 Boise, Idaho   who  requested      then    to  meet  hin at     the Winthrop Hotel in Tacoma on
 July 31, 1947      According     to   then    Arnold  called in arny       intelligence officers
 from Hanilton Field,      California      and one Ceptain Emil H. Smith of United Airlines
 of Seattle , Washingtonto attend this meeting                    Dahl and Chrisman maintained
 they told the intelligence officers Captain Davidson,                   Lieutenant     Brow , Kenneth
 Arnold and   Emil I. Smith exactly how they got the rock formations                     and that   they
 had no connection    with   any flying discs .          Dahl    and Chrisman    stated    that they then
 furnished  somne of the rock       formation      to the intelligence officers          45 samples .


              Captain Davidson and Lieutenant          Brovm left   Tacoma , Washington in a B-25
 to return    to Hamilton Field,      California about      2.30 4.M. August 1, 1947 , and were
 killed   when   their plane    crashed at Kelso, Washington, after the left engine
 burned    out an exhaust    stack which    in turn caught      the left wing on fire which caused
 it   to     break        off     The crew chief and          each   officer parachuted        to   safety.
                     Ernie      Vogel,    an    Associated    Press wireman    at   Tacoma     advised that      two   or

 three days after                the   flying disc story started he           contacted Dahl         to   check the story

                                   Colonel DONALD SPRINGER,
                        On August 27, 1947, Lt.            4-2, Fourth Air
   Force ,Hamilton Field, California,advised that the Area Intelligence Require-
   ments          Division Office              of   the Assistant Chief        of Staff,     4-2 Headquarters, re-
   quested that         WILLIAL RHOADS of Phoenix, Arizona , be completely inter-
                                  one
   viewed in connection      with a report that  he had seen   on July 7, 1947,    what
   he believed   were   flying discs.   RHOADS  1s supposed  to have   taken  several   pic -
   tures  of the discs with a 620 Box Camera.

                        A previous report of this reported sighting of flying discs                             was
     forwarded           the Bureau on August 8, 1947.
                Colonel SPRINGER has advised                          that GEORGE F        FUGATEJr., ,    an
     intelligence agent   of A-2 stationed                       at   Long B ach,     California, would arrive
   in Phoenix, Arizona,on or about                    September 2,819447,
                                                                       and would                    be    instructed
   to       contact          the Phoenix Field Division Office.
                        In       accordance
                          with Bureau Bulletin No.      42, Series 1947, it 1s
     felt      that        the Phoenix Field Division should
                            an
                                  agent from                       interview WILLIAM
   RHOADS at 4333 North lth Street, Phoenix,      if RHOADS has  not already been
   interviewed . Colonel SPRINGER indicated    that Kr . FUGATE should           ,
                                                                             if possible,
   sit       in    on       this interview.
                        A
               copy of the report      from A-2 dated August 4, 1947, along with three
   photographic prints of the pictures allegedly taken by Mr _     RHOADS     ,
                                                                           is being
   enclosed for the Phoenix Field Division.

  While the               impression         was   still

together and made rough sketches
of what they had seen: These, in
turn; were the basis for the artist'$
conception of the strange affair by
Vic Goertzen of Twin Falls for the
Times-News.
  "It   was al1            one    color__sort      of
light sky blue with       a red, tubular
fiery glow at the side of the     top
         Urie continued.
    The   canyon    floor  is rough at
that ,particular point, and       it rode
up and down over the hills and hol-
       at    speed indicating some
type of control faster than the
reflexes of man. It          my opinion
that it is guided by instruments and
must be powered by ,atomic energy,
as it made very little noise~just
S-W-i-S-h  as it passed by_
  Urie described the size as about
                      10 feet high and
10 feet wide, giving it an oblong
shape: It might be described as look
ing like an inverted pie-plate Or
broad-brimmed straw hat that had
been compressed from    two sides
  Pressed' for his candid opinion of
just what it              was, Urie said that he
was convinced that there                    was some-
thing to this flying saucer situation.
"Iknow   number of the people who
have also seen them and       know
that they re not just imagining
something or trying to get their

mented:
  "Ido know that it scared the boys
and made me feel pretty uneasy ,
he added.
  Tracing down & rumor that Cou
Commissioner Hawkins had seen      an

home.
  "Yes; I did"     he replied without

been skeptical all along until IsaW
it with my oun eyes:       can't say
         was, but I can say there s
something in the air.
   Hawkins related that while at
Salmon dam Wednesday morning;
   sound resembling the echo                       of
motor caused him                    to look upward,
and there he               saw      GwO circular ob-
jects that reflected light. They were
(traveling at    great speed and
higher than most airplanes, accord-
Ungt0 Hawkins:
   Aside from              this_ he declined to
add details. except to say,                    "There'$
something in the air.
   His general description, howeve
corresponded closely to those of
hundreds of persons          reported
seeing flying saucers after Kenneth
 Arnold,        Boise            businessman,          had
touched off the deluge by telling of
coming uponnine disc-like objects
while he was flying in his private
plane in Washington.
   Thereafter, the nation became In-
creasingly flying                 saucer     conscious,
creating & state of mind that made
it possible for four                 'teen age Twin
Falls boys           to     cause        mild sensa -
tion when they built a model flying
disc and tossed AInto     local yard
July 10. This was subseuently re-
vealed as   hoax; following investi-
gation by the army and FBI
  Thereafter the  saucer reports
tapered off into & few scattered in-
cidents until the question was re-
vived by this week'$ occurrences:
   Speculation   has ranged from
mention that the discs could be
army    or navy guided missile experi-

 try. to         some       thing out of                this
 world"

of 1113 S_ T. 102nd Street     Seattle, and their version       of the object corresponded
with that           of NICHOLS and STANLEY .

              Contact with Mr.    THOMAS E. GERMAIN _of' the Seattle Weather            Bureau Office
at Boe ing Field, revealed      that weather      balloons    are released every     six hours
from his    field comencing at 1:30        a.m.     The balloons     according to Mr GERMAIN
                                                                                           _            ,
ere  of a white gum color      and reflect      the light    of the sun at      high &ltitude
Although Mr_ GERMAIN could not understand why a balloon cou ld have been sighted
at 4 pam. , he nevertheless       was certain that ,      from the description     of the object
seen   by the  se  people it   must   heve been either       one  of his balloons    or one    released
from       some     other          area        in     the vicinity
                    No further                 investigation            is   be ing conducted               in    this    matter      by this
office .
